# Explorer's Index — 517 Cave-Dive Spine

Generated from `subsurface (1).ssrf`. Cave dives were identified only where the dive description begins with `Total Cave Time:`. The canonical cave-dive number is the number in brackets on that first line; the dive software number is retained only as reference metadata.

## How to use this index

- **Story** is pre-filled from the dive-log notes when the log contains narrative detail. Blank entries need memory, photos, survey files, or later notes added.
- **Lesson, Humour, Wonder, Links** are lightly pre-filled using conservative keyword cues from the log. Treat these as prompts, not final interpretation.
- Add manuscript candidates by marking `Book relevance: ★★★★★`, `Chapter candidate: yes`, or linking an entry to a chapter note.

## Summary

- Cave dives identified: **480**
- Cave-dive sequence: **#2–#517**
- Date range: **2019-08-22–2026-05-13**

### Cave Dive #11 — 2019-11-16 — Allendale East, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `51:40`, max depth `28.0 m`, buddy `Matthew Aisbett (Matt)`, Subsurface dive `270`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 535mins (11)
- **Story:** First tunnel exploration. 
Used 2x12l cylinders - too large for reversing out. Got stuck, needed to fully remove right cylinder and dragged to get back out.
- **Lesson:** Restrictions can turn equipment choices into exit problems; keep kit appropriate and preserve options.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** Potential comedy in hindsight: the undignified physics of trying to reverse a cave diver out of a restriction.; Potential comedy: the cave giving brutally honest feedback on equipment choices.
- **Wonder:** Discovery / first-look exploration.
- **Links:** Allendale East, Mount Gambier, Matthew Aisbett (Matt), Risk, Humour, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})

### Cave Dive #26 — 2019-12-16 — Allendale East, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `72:00`, max depth `27.0 m`, buddy `Matthew Aisbett (Matt)`, Subsurface dive `311`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 1179 mins (26)
- **Story:** Diving 4x7Ls

First attempt at diving with 4x7's, dumping 2x7's near tunnel entrance, heading in (3:00) til rule of thirds (18:00 = 15mins in tunnel), backing out (20:00), full cylinder swap & back in on fresh 7's (25:00 = 5mins to swap) till thirds (39:00 = 14mins in tunnel), re-rig used 7's & head to deco (42:30 = 3:30 to re-rig).

Cleared deco at 69:30 but stuck around for an extra few minutes waiting for Matt's Teric to clear.
- **Lesson:** Restrictions can turn equipment choices into exit problems; keep kit appropriate and preserve options.
- **Humour:** Potential comedy in hindsight: the undignified physics of trying to reverse a cave diver out of a restriction.
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Allendale East, Mount Gambier, Matthew Aisbett (Matt), Risk, Humour
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:** 
- **Follow-up notes:** 

### Cave Dive #27 — 2019-12-17 — Allendale East, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `76:30`, max depth `26.0 m`, buddy `Matthew Aisbett (Matt)`, Subsurface dive `312`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 1256 mins (27)
- **Story:** Acting as airbus for Matt while he worked in the tunnel - Matt using 4x7L cylinder setup

Explored rock pile and low/silty flattener while waiting for Matt - silt was incredibly thick and started to have rocks roll down on top of me, so backed out and stayed near the entrance to the tunnel to keep an eye on Matt. 

After Matt swapped cylinders and headed back into the tunnel I clipped on his partially used 7's over my 12's to speed exit from the cave to start decompression - gave back during 6m stop
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Allendale East, Mount Gambier, Matthew Aisbett (Matt), Risk, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})
- **Book relevance:**

### Cave Dive #28 — 2019-12-18 — Fossil Cave (West), Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `54:10`, max depth `15.0 m`, buddy `Matthew Aisbett (Matt)`, Subsurface dive `313`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 1310 mins (28)
- **Story:** Following previously established line. Took tools and worked on restriction at 15m - significant progress breaking away & cutting rock, but not quite large enough to squeeze through. Evidence of intermittent flow - work site becomes blindingly silty, but every few minutes silt suddenly clears with water appearing to come out of restriction and passed diver at work site.

Couldn't find guideline on departure from work site - got tangled around my legs while backing out of the horizontal corridor to the worksite. Completely entangled by the time I backed out of the corridor - spent 6 minutes trying to untangle myself before realising I was pulling the remaining line off the unsecured spindle. 

MUST secure guideline all the way to the work site, but then CUT and remove the line (and tangled spindle) from anywhere near the worksite and the tools. For future guidelines they need to be used to speed entry through known areas, but must be cut and loose line removed from any future worksite with new areas explored on a reel (NOT with a loose spindle of "established" line)
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; Line awareness and navigation discipline matter more as comfort increases.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Fossil Cave (West), Mount Gambier, Matthew Aisbett (Matt), Risk, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:** 
- **Follow-up notes:**

### Cave Dive #31 — 2020-01-01 — Kilsby Sinkhole, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `49:20`, max depth `50.0 m`, buddy `Lachlan Trott, Simon Backman`, Subsurface dive `329`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 1427 mins (31)
- **Story:** Exploring 37m restriction at Kilsbys.

Lachlan lead through, then Simon, with me at rear. Allowed the other two to move ahead through restriction before investigating deeper section. 

Followed lead down to 50m and could see at least another 5m of depth, but feeling very narked while diving without a guideline and essentially by myself, so turned at 50m and came back up through 27m restriction to join others. Followed Lachlan through a few more swimthroughs, hung out at the back of the sinkhole for abit, then hit TTS=20mins and headed back towards descent line to begin deco.
- **Lesson:** The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Kilsby Sinkhole, Mount Gambier, Lachlan Trott, Simon Backman, Risk, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})

### Cave Dive #34 — 2020-01-11 — Mudhole, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `22:20`, max depth `22.0 m`, buddy `Matthew Aisbett (Matt)`, Subsurface dive `337`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 1539 mins (34)
- **Story:** Matt leading into Mudhole. Through to third chamber, Matt led line all the way behind Coffin Rock (but I didn't see it).

Took wire saw and tried cutting rock I knew was blocking way beneath Coffin Rock - cut through quickly but no way to know if it had cleared the path. Backed out, found Matt and the line, then followed it back down behind Coffin Rock to find/retrieve reel. 

Matt lost line while I was retrieving reel - took a compass bearing, found third chamber restriction, then found line passing through it. In future will take a bearing on compass after passing any particularly silty restriction so it's easier to find again in the case of line loss.
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; Line awareness and navigation discipline matter more as comfort increases.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Mudhole, Mount Gambier, Matthew Aisbett (Matt), Risk, Teamwork

### Cave Dive #47 — 2020-01-29 — Pines, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `81:40`, max depth `43.0 m`, buddy `Lachlan Trott`, Subsurface dive `360`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 2348 mins (47) 
- **Story:** CCR with Lachy. 
Explored Bunyip tunnel, had a dig at depth trying to follow a lead. Followed line up into CCR - tried to tie on to explore further using reel borrowed from Rob, but lock knob rusted in place and wouldn't spool so decided against going further. 

Definitely need to return to CCR without messing around at depth and accruing deco. Use a simple reel to get down to fixed line in adv cave section, use simple spools between jumps to move quickly and cleanly on direct line to CCR. Once at CCR, tie on with exploration reel to end of established line and head in 10 o'clock direction (deep section with potential leads)
- **Lesson:** Exploration sometimes means slow, ridiculous persistence rather than heroic discovery.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** Potential comedy: cave exploration as underwater gardening with worse visibility.
- **Wonder:**

### Cave Dive #48 — 2020-02-05 — Ewens Ponds, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `53:30`, max depth `18.0 m`, buddy `Sam Brookes`, Subsurface dive `362`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 2402 mins (48)
- **Story:** Dive with Sam to help his mental health. Wore wetsuit and took small sledge & chisel to work on cave entrance - broke through limestone *very* easily, so no-mounted and squeezed through with camera. 

Followed water flow from small entrance room into slightly larger room (restriction requires swung 7l cylinder). Follow flow through a Northerly tunnel that bends East, opens into a low flattened room with downward sloping flattener heading to the South. *VERY* strong flow coming out of sloping flattener - followed flattener down to 17m but starting to get concerned at high flow and only having a single 7l cylinder. 

Took photos the whole way, but need to return with Paralenz and chisel/hammer in order to push down further into sloping flattener - appears to bell out slightly which may be possible to push through (potentially major aquifer underneath creating huge flow)
- **Lesson:** 
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Ewens Ponds, Mount Gambier, Sam Brookes, Risk
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:** 
- **Follow-up notes:** 

### Cave Dive #49 — 2020-02-06 — Ewens Ponds, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `45:00`, max depth `19.0 m`, buddy `Ryan Kaczkowski, Solo`, Subsurface dive `363`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 2447 mins (49)
- **Story:** Scariest dive of my life. 
Borrowed long hose from R2R, borrowed no-mount harness from Ryan. 
Arrived before Ryan, geared up & headed to 3rd pond alone.

Slight leak on short hose reg and weird breathing (post-breath pressure) during descent towards restriction - cylinder turned off and back on half turn. Removed fins, unclipped cylinders, removed harness with canister light on belt, dropped feet first into restriction.

Initial restriction felt tighter than day before - concerned about arms seeming to be trapped above head during wriggle, unable to push loose long-hose regulator back between lips. Restriction took longer to prepare for, pass, and re-rig than expected. Tried to slow breathing down by taking time to put harness and cylidners back on, but took a long time to sort out gear, then realised after fully re-rigging I'd need to remove a cylinder again to pass 2nd entrance restriction.

Passed 2nd restriction, and tried to tie in a reel. Loose rock & no tie-offs meant another 5mins wasted trying to secure line, before giving up and deciding to push on in clear water without running guideline.

Pushed through tunnel and straight into low flattener restriction, heading down slope. Stopped for a few minutes to inspect both an unusual rock and the "drop" at end of restriction, trying to determine if safe to push further ahead - likely room to push on against current, but concerned by number of failures and reaching turn pressure - turned dive.

Long hose regulator ripped out of mouth by current as soon as I turned perpendicular to flow. Reached for short hose, found it'd been ripped out of necklace. Reached back for long hose flapping in flow - long hose now breathing wet, bordering on panic. Find short hose behind shoulder, shove in mouth - no air, cylinder must have been rolled off during wriggle down flattener. Switch back to wet long-hose, turn on left cylinder, swap from long to short hose - first full, dry breath in several minutes.

Spend several minutes getting breathing back under control on short hose while edging back up through flattener. Exit flattener, back through tunnel, through 2nd entrance restriction, into entrance chamber. Ryan is now in 3rd pond just outside restriction, left a long hose leading into restriction for me to use but fed into side of restriction rather hanging down into it where I could use it to get out.

Pass cylinders and gear out of restriction - Ryan initially grabs cylinder with long hose and goes to move it away, but I grab it as I'm breathing from it & stress levels increase. Start passing restriction, but get jammed halfway through - I've not removed the harness, and the top of the canister light on the belt has stuck under a lip of limestone. Bordering on panic again, start breathing hard and long hose regulator starts breathing wet again. Can't push my way out because of canister light, can't go backwards because I'm breathing too hard my chest has expanded, can't reach my face to push loose regulator back into my mouth because my arms are trapped over my head by limestone. 

Spend several minutes in near-panic, trying to breathe while stuck. Suddenly felt free, and could easily reach down to move canister light passed limestone lip - later learned Ryan heard two of my ribs break underwater, explaining the sudden freedom. Popped out of restriction, and rested nearby getting my breath back as Ryan attempted to wriggle through entrance restriction himself (unsucessful). 

Eventually drifted back to the surface together and had long debrief on water-side about what had just happened. 
*****
In future, need to take my time and be more aware of my emotional state and stress levels when getting in the water. Don't use gear that's unfamiliar (harness) or unreliable (long-hose) when cave diving at the best of times, but *especially* when doing push dives that involve no-mount restrictions. 

Eager to return to Ewens Cave, however must be done:
- At a much slower & methodical pace (check for valve roll-offs & that regs are in place before entering restrictions)
- Using equipment I'm familiar with & trust (no bubbles, no weird breathing, always able to reach my mouth)
- After chipping away at entrance restriction to make fractionally wider (chisel where ribs broke)

******
Future Development
- Chisel more of entrance restriction
Target area where my ribs were when they broke. Also need to reduce limestone around shoulders & elbows so it's possible to push a regulator back into mouth. Recommend chipping out two ledges on Eastern side of restriction's tighest point (image marked on google photos) to clear right ribcage easier. Also clear away rock below smallest part of restriction to make movement below tighest point easier.

- Spend more time in entrance chamber to become familiar & clear away rubble 
Room inside entrance is limited making passing restriction difficult, so any loose rock that can be removed from directly below restriction will make things easier. Loose rubble can be thrown to far end of entrance chamber through the impassable restriction at the southern end. Take your time in the entrance chamber and get to understand it well - look for potential ways to make it safer/easier.

- Clear away rubble from restriction into second chamber
Second restriction is not small, but requires swinging a 7l to pass through. Loose rock there cannot be tied on to, so anything removed will improve the restriction. Pass through restriction, then pull away rock from opposite side so that it ends up in larger second chamber.

- Run line after second restriction down through tunnel, into flattener, and beyond. 
Start guideline out of view from anyone poking their head into the entrance restriction, however important to run established line from second room onwards. High flow & no natural tie-offs may mean bolts/staples are required. *Use old blue reel with yellow/green line? Leave it down there? Follow up with blue line and staples? Measure lengths, depths, and line headings*

- Recover unusual rectangular rock from bottom of flattener
Weird patterning. Grab it, bring it out of flattener to the tunnel (or even second chamber), then return to flattener to push on. Photograph underwater and also bring back to surface for later study.

- Inspect area at bottom of flattener more throughly
Wear your paralenz. Take a GoPro on a selfie stick (with light attached) to push down into area below. Consider dropping old blue reel into "drop" below to see where it goes (under ledge?) before you follow it. Pick up some limestone and crush it, looking where the flow comes from (likely to be from south)
- **Lesson:** Restrictions can turn equipment choices into exit problems; keep kit appropriate and preserve options.; Gas planning only helps if it is constantly rechecked against the actual dive.
- **Humour:** Potential comedy in hindsight: the undignified physics of trying to reverse a cave diver out of a restriction.
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Ewens Ponds, Mount Gambier, Ryan Kaczkowski, Solo, Risk, Humour, Teamwork, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:** 
- **Follow-up notes:**

### Cave Dive #113 — 2021-05-23 — Pines, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `92:20`, max depth `35.0 m`, buddy `Martin Slater, Burak Uslu`, Subsurface dive `493`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 6521 minutes (113)
- **Story:** Total Cave CCR Time: 1808 minutes (32)
Total Sidewinder CCR time: 2869 minutes (56)

Planned dive to Surprise Tunnel. Burak pushed on to CCR, turned dive at bottom of rockpile. I had a quick look for more cookies in the 31m side tunnel, then followed out to Dark Room. Swam passed Burak & Martin in Dark Room as they were messing with jump spool, and headed to start of established line. 

Waited by the reel for them, saw lights approaching but they never appeared - headed back down the line to find Burak retrieving another jump out of Surprise Tunnel (?!?). All headed back to main cavern, then checked deco - I was clear, but they had +40mins and no deco gas (?!?). Hung with them for awhile figuring out what to do, realised my bailout gas was 37% - gave each of them a reg and hung for awhile as they cut down their deco. 

After 5mins Burak wrote "Have O2 in car" and gave me his car key underwater. I dropped my sidemount cylinders so they could keep using them, then hung onto rocks to avoid popping to the surface as I made my way to the stairs. Ran to his car, unlocked it and grabed his O2 stage, then hurled it into the watr for them to use. 

Once they cleared deco and surfaced, it turned out they hadn't seen me go passed them in the Dark Room, and had gone BACK into the Surprise Tunnel to look for me (?!?). Both were upset with me because I felt I'd been changing the order of the dive, but the reality is they shouldn't have been going that far in without a deco plan, and certainly shouldn't have been going back into a cave when they'd already exceeded thirds. Bit of a shitfight, but glad we all got out safely in the end.
- **Lesson:** Gas planning only helps if it is constantly rechecked against the actual dive.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Pines, Mount Gambier, Martin Slater, Burak Uslu, Risk, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:** 
- **Follow-up notes:** 

### Cave Dive #114 — 2021-05-26 — Ewens Ponds, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `39:30`, max depth `19.0 m`, buddy `Lukasz Lipinski`, Subsurface dive `494`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 6561 minutes (114)
- **Story:** Exploration of Ewens Cave using wetsuit, no-lift harness, no fins, sump light, 2x7s & AL40 stage. Lukasz hung around the 3rd pond practicing skills while I was inside.

Super easy entry - lifted righthand rocks out, dropped stage near entrance, removed cylinders and dropped short hose down the hole, followed down with the long hose. Pushed straight into 2nd room & re-rigged in 
Tied yellow line from blue reel onto rock just inside 2nd room, then ran line into tunnel and down flattener. Flow not as severe as I remember - pushed down flattener (which felt very tight) to 19m, tied line around rock and cut, clipping reel onto newly established line. Wriggled around at bottom of the flattener for awhile looking for a way forward, but all felt too tight and nervous with flow. 

Grabbed one of the patterned rocks from below the flattener and brought it all the way back up to the start of the established line, dropped it there, then realised I'd left the reel clipped to the other end. Swam back down the line, recovered reel, and turned around back to entrance. 

Moved into the 1st room awhile to do some cleaning - pushing loose rocks out into the 2nd room and clearing the restriction between the two rooms. Recovered the mallet and chisel and placed them at the start of the established line. Placed short hose cylinder on 1st room ledge, then pushed out of cave with long hose cylinder ahead of me. Re-rigged, replaced righthand rocks, exited water.

Need to return with MNemo (one dive) and Paralenz (separate dive). Limited exploration potential beyond flattener already reached - any further push will require chiselling of flattener.
- **Lesson:** Restrictions can turn equipment choices into exit problems; keep kit appropriate and preserve options.; Exploration becomes more valuable when it is measured, mapped, and made legible for others.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** Making the invisible cave visible through survey and mapping.
- **Links:** Ewens Ponds, Mount Gambier, Lukasz Lipinski, Risk, Survey, Teamwork, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:** 
- **Follow-up notes:**

### Cave Dive #116 — 2021-06-06 — Ewens Ponds, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `38:00`, max depth `19.0 m`, buddy `Solo`, Subsurface dive `496`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 6681 minutes (116)
- **Story:** Took GoPro and Paralenz into 3rd pond cave. No major issues, however flow out of cave considerably higher than dive 10 days earlier (rain?). Recovered large rock from 19m bedding plane to split/share with others.

Further exploration is unlikely - any further push will require chiselling inside down-angled, high-flow, no-mount flattener. GoPro footage also suggests that there is very little beyond the current restriction to push into. 

Next Ewens dive to focus on surveying established line and (potentially) a test chisel of flattener, but unlikely to be conducting any further Ewens cave dives after survey.

### Cave Dive #135 — 2021-07-11 — Tank Cave, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `70:40`, max depth `13.0 m`, buddy `Martin Slater`, Subsurface dive `520`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 7714 minutes (135)
- **Story:** Total Tank Dives: 19

[Group 2] Exploring R. Swam to R1 and dropped tools near rockpile. Followed line around to R9 clockwise, then looped back to R9 from the other direction. 

Jumped just east of R9 towards a tunnel to the north - spotted line running into a smaller tunnel on left side, so tied on and followed it. Martin couldn't follow because of silt & restrictions, but pushed on to find another room with line running West. Turned around to find Martin because we hadn't discussed anything like this.

Moved back to R1 rockpile to try breaking through. Chisel tip too fine and rock too soft, so will need wide chisel for future attempts
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.

- **Wonder:**

### Cave Dive #140 — 2021-07-19 — Ewens Ponds, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `36:30`, max depth `18.0 m`, buddy `Solo`, Subsurface dive `525`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 7902 minutes (140)
- **Story:** Final dive in Ewens Cave, super high flow because of rains. Forgot reel, so ran survey from start of established line @ ~11m. Ran Mnemo from one end of established line to the other (rockpile to bottom of flattener) then returned to start of line. 

Clipped off MNemo near start, picked up hammer & chisel, then placed arrows on line heading back into flattener - clear yellow arrow mid-line @ ~13m, glow-in-the-dark arrow placed at end of line @ ~18m. Attempted to chisel parts of rock as moving along line, but limited impact and concerns about shaking rocks loose. 

Returned to entrance restriction with MNemo & Hammer/Chisel, then attempted to push several large rocks making entrance into rubble room difficult - rocks moved *very* easily. Last rock made rubble pile shift too, and initially had major concerns that shift had blocked exit out of cave - realised rock shift had disorientated me, and entrance restriction was still clear. 

Exited as quickly as possible, forgot to replace cover rock over entrance restriction.
- **Lesson:** Exploration becomes more valuable when it is measured, mapped, and made legible for others.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** Making the invisible cave visible through survey and mapping.
- **Links:** Ewens Ponds, Mount Gambier, Solo, Risk, Survey
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:** 
- **Follow-up notes:** 

### Cave Dive #141 — 2021-07-20 — Fossil Cave (East), Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `77:50`, max depth `20.0 m`, buddy `Solo`, Subsurface dive `526`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 7902 minutes (141)
- **Story:** Initial survey from Fossil East through Rasp and around to Last Chamber. 

Survey took considerably longer than expected - survey in took 50mins, but only 10mins to swim back out. Extended time below 18m meant ~15mins of deco. Climbed out cold and tired, raced back home before COVID lockdown began at 6pm
- **Lesson:** Exploration becomes more valuable when it is measured, mapped, and made legible for others.

### Cave Dive #144 — 2021-08-07 — Alleyn's Cave, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `28:30`, max depth `11.3 m`, buddy `Solo`, Subsurface dive `528`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 7999 minutes (144)
- **Story:** Relatively comfortable dive. Water level in cave higher than usual, so no need to crawl over Bryan's Beach. Ran new, heavier line into 2nd restriction near where GoPro dropped. Used trowel for awhile to clear onward tunnel, but silt-out is extreme and tie-off for yellow line came free while I was holding it and decided to bug out with trowel. 

Dropped trowel in 2nd sump just passed junction (right at 1st restriction). Retrieved fluro arrow from 1st sump and placed it near junction in 2nd sump (will replace on next dive). Inspected walls in 1st sump - thought I identified claw marks, but obviously just tracks from water running down walls from above.

Access through restrictions needs to be improved for safety. Future dives need to focus on clearing mud/silt from floor of 1st and 2nd restrictions before pushing into tunnel with dropped GoPro.
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Alleyn's Cave, Mount Gambier, Solo, Risk, Teamwork

### Cave Dive #150 — 2021-08-29 — Pines, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `48:10`, max depth `35.6 m`, buddy `Solo`, Subsurface dive `538`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 8441 minutes (150)
- **Story:** Dedicated dig in CCR left tunnel. Used Jetsuit and helmet - felt slightly out of balance diving different setup, but ideal for this dive. Slight disorientation setting up for dig while trying to do tie-off upside-down, and more disorientation after leaving dig site and exploring entrance side of rockpile.

Significant silt pouring through rock in Surprise Tunnel around 30m (gas switch) after digging. Significant silting means it's unclear if progress is being made at dig site, but definitely felt like a lot of movement of soft silt/mud (still very hard rock present that will require chiselling).
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; Gas planning only helps if it is constantly rechecked against the actual dive.; Exploration sometimes means slow, ridiculous persistence rather than heroic discovery.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** Potential comedy: cave exploration as underwater gardening with worse visibility.
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Pines, Mount Gambier, Solo, Risk, Humour, Teamwork, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})

### Cave Dive #164 — 2021-10-20 — Englebrecht Cave (East), Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `14:50`, max depth `13.8 m`, buddy `Andii Russo`, Subsurface dive `559`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 9390 minutes (164)
- **Story:** Pushing the Eastern sumps. Andii was wearing a drysuit so stayed in main chamber.

Found wetnote sign in front of the beach - mostly unreadable but finished with "Keep Clear". Tied orange line around a rock in the dry room passed the beach, then laid line through next sump and up another beach. Wriggled down into next sump to find silt rolling down the steep incline to my front-right. Followed silt avalanche around bend into new tunnel (!) that ran down to 14m. Reel ran out line halfway along tunnel, turned to drag it back but found large slab had dropped behind me. Followed line around collapse back to air, dumped reel to deal with next time, followed surface line back to Andii in main chamber.

Need to organise major equipment dive to deliver hammer, chisel, lots of extra line, line staples. Future dives will require helmet with backup lights, LX20, Paralenz, MNemo.
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; Exploration becomes more valuable when it is measured, mapped, and made legible for others.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** Discovery / first-look exploration.; Making the invisible cave visible through survey and mapping.
- **Links:** Englebrecht Cave (East), Mount Gambier, Andii Russo, Risk, Wonder, Survey, Teamwork, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})

- **Chapter candidate:**

### Cave Dive #184 — 2021-12-01 — Englebrecht Cave (East), Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `24:37`, max depth `13.6 m`, buddy `Solo`, Subsurface dive `586`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 10534 minutes (184)
- **Story:** Pushing the "Grimm" sumps in Englebrecht East

Climbed the beach into "Iron Henry" and dropped helmet and fins to pass the short sump. Down into next sump, collected loose line and started resetting orange "Gretel" line running down into 270deg tunnel. Line reached to 11m tunnel, tied on new reel and continued pushing West along tunnel down to 13m. 

Approached breakdown area and found upward chimney leading to a large room (Gingerbread Room) approx 15-20m and 30-40m long and ~5m deep in centre of room. Ran line 3/4 length of room (on 140deg heading) before running out of line. 

Secured line to rock, tied on jump spool and swam further to inspect unusual feature on far left side of room. Feature turned out to be short fissure only 3-4m deep, but spotted significant tunnel below and to right (along of axis of room) running away at 140deg. 

Hit agreed turn time so collected jump spool and swam out checking line on exit. Swim from end of line back to main entrance lake is ~10mins (including climbing rocks in main air chamber and other sumps).

Next dive:
- MUST MOVE ORANGE LINE BACK TO BE HIDDEN BY IRON HENRY SUMP 
- Leave behind helmet (uncomfortable and too big for Iron Henry Sump)
- Get fins through Iron Henry sump (needed for Gingerbread room & beyond)

- Survey to Gingerbread Room with MNemo
- Need extra reels and LOTS of line to push beyond Gingerbread Room
- Run "Hansel" line along fissure sumps
- **Lesson:** Exploration becomes more valuable when it is measured, mapped, and made legible for others.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** Making the invisible cave visible through survey and mapping.
- **Links:** Englebrecht Cave (East), Mount Gambier, Solo, Survey, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:** 
- **Follow-up notes:**

### Cave Dive #252 — 2022-04-13 — Englebrecht Cave (East), Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `43:40`, max depth `20.1 m`, buddy `Simon Backman`, Subsurface dive `662`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 14,503 minutes (252)
- **Story:** Full video survey of EEX covering both Western & Southern lines. 

Checked leads in Big Southern Room on Western side - messy and silty, no obvious potential. 

Pushed camera into lead at the very end of the Southern line - *VERY* high potential for ongoing passage, but will require chisel to break rock ledge blocking the way on.

Based on video analysis of this dive from Lewy, he recommends investigating the strange fissure feature halfway along the Southern line - he suspects it's an intersection point of a NW-SE fracture with an E-W fracture, and there is likely to be ongoing Western tunnel under the broken rock.
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; Exploration becomes more valuable when it is measured, mapped, and made legible for others.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** Making the invisible cave visible through survey and mapping.
- **Links:** Englebrecht Cave (East), Mount Gambier, Simon Backman, Risk, Survey, Teamwork, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})

### Cave Dive #257 — 2022-04-15 — Pines, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `68:00`, max depth `35.6 m`, buddy `Unknown`, Subsurface dive `667`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 14,814 minutes (257)
- **Story:** Dive to CCR, checking leads in main rockpile and side passage. 

Side passage doesn't appear to go anywhere (previous digging has filled with silt) however there is very likely vertical access down the rock pile on the N-NW side of the room (on the right as you enter the CCR) with blue water below. High probability of ongoing tunnel if rocks are moved.

Also inspected area with white line that has potential for pulling a rock in the rockpile - risk is too great, and looking below doesn't show great potential for further access.
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; Exploration sometimes means slow, ridiculous persistence rather than heroic discovery.
- **Humour:** Potential comedy: cave exploration as underwater gardening with worse visibility.
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Pines, Mount Gambier, Unknown, Risk, Humour, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})
- **Book relevance:**

### Cave Dive #346 — 2023-01-27 — Englebrecht Cave (West), Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `52:00`, max depth `9.2 m`, buddy `Chloe Reid, William Passos (Will)`, Subsurface dive `763`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 20618 minutes (346)
- **Story:** Chloe & Will's first dive in Englebrecht's West. Very hot on surface and issues with tour group trying to get to the water. Chloe had issues going into the small stinky entrance hole, and Will lost the line then got entangled during entrance, but then minimal issues oncewe were all in the water. Went to the far Western end, chatted in the air pocket, then swam back (after I checked out the dig on the right side of the Western collapse). Explored the other tunnel near the Western tunnel but found no leads.

Keen to come back and fully explore the side passage that runs South-East from the main air chamber rickpile.
- **Lesson:** Exploration sometimes means slow, ridiculous persistence rather than heroic discovery.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.

### Cave Dive #347 — 2023-01-28 — Iddlebiddy, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `39:40`, max depth `19.8 m`, buddy `Chloe Reid, William Passos (Will)`, Subsurface dive `764`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 20658 minutes (347)
- **Story:** Laying line in #5 side tunnel while Chloe & Will explored far end and took photos. 
Used silt pegs for entrance, line laid through largest passage but not enough to create junction for smaller passage near entrance. 
Tied on jump and explored smaller passage again - not any chance of extension but still worthy of line in the future.

Found an area to dig not far from junction and to the left of the large passage - clear hole in the wall which leads into a room with some silt from initial entrance area. Well worth taking tools in the future and digging out the soft lower rock and pushing through to see if there's more.
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; Exploration sometimes means slow, ridiculous persistence rather than heroic discovery.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.

- **Metadata:** duration `90:40`, max depth `11.2 m`, buddy `Martin Slater`, Subsurface dive `765`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 20749 minutes (348)
- **Story:** Major issues with stage regulator on the way out - no ziptie on mouth piece and hose too short, so kept pulling out and inhaling water. 
Dumped stage at N7, then headed into new tunnel to recover Matt's reel and explore. 

Found a series of "loops" where small tunnels loop back on each other. Found way out through loops to S11 - will need to lay fresh line to make sense of everything that's going on in that area, but doesn't look very promising as a way on.

- **Metadata:** duration `53:20`, max depth `18.8 m`, buddy `Martin Slater`, Subsurface dive `791`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 22202 minutes (374)
- **Story:** Martin having issues trying to push into new air chamber - after a chat back at the entrance I went in ahead of him while he kept trying to get through.

Pushed into new chamber and laid line into lead on NW corner. Small clay tunnel leads to large sphere-shaped room (1m at top, 16m at bottom) with a drop in NW corner that opens into a low flat series of tunnels at 18m. Tunnels have very low roof over huge clay blocks, with less than a meter clearance between roof and blocks.

- **Metadata:** duration `32:30`, max depth `18.6 m`, buddy `Solo`, Subsurface dive `855`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 25496 minutes (435)
- **Story:** Pushing Iddlebiddy Extention to try and connect with Jam Cave. Pushed end of line in IDX, up into soft material while looking for light from Martin diving from the other side in Jam. 

Total whiteout and no lights seen from Martin. Reached 11.3m (deepest in Jam is 11.8m) but soft material feels like it may collapse - further exploration needs to be done from Jam Cave side, with potential to find connection by lighting with dropped cylume sticks.

### Cave Dive #436 — 2024-06-22 — Pines, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `43:10`, max depth `35.1 m`, buddy `William Passos (Will), Martin Slater`, Subsurface dive `856`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 25539 minutes (436)
- **Story:** Pushing the new lead in the CCR. Used A80 to Dark Room, A40 to CCR, then 2x7s inside lead.

Pushed into lead at 16m, found angled slot at 21m which clears very quickly - flow coming out of slot, with room beyond restriction at ~22-23m. Very exciting discovery - need to return with tools to widen slot, access room, and follow flow to push beyond.

Jumped back in afterwards to test DPVs. First dive with mine failed quickly - motor ran briefly, then stopped with unknown error message on LCD. 2nd dive with Chloe's DPV worked perfectly.
- **Lesson:** The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** 
- **Wonder:** Discovery / first-look exploration.
- **Links:** Pines, Mount Gambier, William Passos (Will), Martin Slater, Risk, Wonder, Teamwork, [Exploration]({{< relref "posts/exploration.md" >}})
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:**

- **Metadata:** duration `57:00`, max depth `35.5 m`, buddy `Simon Backman`, Subsurface dive `870`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 26334 minutes (450) [Perdix broken - diving with NiTek Duo & manual logging]
- **Story:** Return to new lead in CCR. Relatively easy through dig restriction - new area ("Crazy Wombat Room") is very silty, but lot of obvious leads heading down. Some options to go upwards too, although restriction is already at 21m and phreatic passage is most likely at 34 - need to head down to find anything substantial.

Concerns about line placement and tie-offs - recommend taking a reel that can be left to avoid laying/recovering every time the area is explored. 
Obvious flow experienced while digging, so need to establish where flow is coming from inside Crazy Wombat Room

### Cave Dive #451 — 2024-09-08 — Pines, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `49:00`, max depth `35.1 m`, buddy `Corey Auld`, Subsurface dive `872`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 26383 minutes (451)
- **Story:** Exploring new section of Pines, first dive with new Perdix

Getting easier to squeeze in but still wetsuit & 7s. Felt cold after drinking night before, so definitely distracted. 
Regardless, couldn't see an obvious way onwards in there - definitely need to run line just as a semi-permanant guide, but very scary diving through a potentially unstable rockpile. Swam up to see if there is a 2nd way in from the CCR but no luck. 

Will take GoPro on selfie stick next time and lay green line (leave reel behind). Very obvious that water flow is keeping restriction clear, but not obvious where flow is coming from.
Use GoPro on next dive to see down slot behind rock that faces you after clearing restriction and looking ahead - very tight, but only obvious place to jam camera down and see if it opens up.
- **Lesson:** The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.

- **Metadata:** duration `65:50`, max depth `35.1 m`, buddy `William Passos (Will), Matt Gebert`, Subsurface dive `878`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 26610 minutes (455)
- **Story:** Testing X4 360 camera in cave environment. Will led, me in the middle, Matt tail end. 
Matt was having issues with transmitter and turned back at bottom of chimney to CCR. Filmed all the way from entrance lake through to restriction into new section (Crazy Wombat Room/Pile). Reel jammed inside restriction, caused issues and needed to back, fix, then try again. 

Filmed all through rockpile, following line previously laid. Rechecked very exciting hole in rockpile that may lead down and onwards (into flow) - camera shows there is definitely space back there.

Next dive need to entirely re-lay line - run reel through restriction, swim forward to retrieve exisiting white line, then lay orange line beyond restriction to potential lead. Use retrived white line to run from start of orange line back towards CCR to reduce distance - will allow just a jump spool from CCR fixed line, rather than main reel.

Finished dive by fitting X4 to DPV and roaming around Pines cavern zone to test footage.

### Cave Dive #459 — 2024-12-28 — Pines, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `73:20`, max depth `34.9 m`, buddy `Solo`, Subsurface dive `882`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 26823 minutes (459)
- **Story:** 2nd dive in new section beyond CCR. Recovered white line reel, laid orange line through 3/4 of previously discovered tunnel. 

Lots of hanging silt in first room just after restriction that requires swinging a 12 - aka "Portal". Checked all the walls but couldn't find any obvious leads in low vis. 

Turned south into the East-West "Gallery" - suspected fissure line, with passage to east leading back towards CCR. Lots of potential floor holes around the where the line bends South to West, with very promising hole heading further south out of galler.

Passage is full of roof collapse, with potential leads heading north which need to be properly inspected. Tied off orange line after dip from 22m to 27m (tied at 27m) with multiple leads around collapse. Followed white line up collapse to reel at 16m and recovered it - potential lead heading north out of collpase, but may require digging to get through. 

Hit thirds and returned through portal - handsaw outside portal on right hand side, need to use it to open portal up (so swinging 12 not required) on next dive. Also need to fix orange line through portal, as it'spartially blocking the way out.
- **Lesson:** Visibility is a temporary privilege; procedures need to work when the cave disappears.; Exploration sometimes means slow, ridiculous persistence rather than heroic discovery.

### Cave Dive #475 — 2025-03-01 — Pines, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `71:00`, max depth `34.6 m`, buddy `Corey Auld, Matt Gebert, Darren Mitchell (Daz)`, Subsurface dive `898`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 27997 minutes (475)
- **Story:** Corey & Matt, Darren & I diving the new section. Corey & Matt left 10mins ahead for Darren & I, with Corey racing ahead to check his lead while Matt attempted to get through Gadi Gap again. Saw Matt in CCR (no success) so Darz headed in first and got through both restrictions, meeting Corey at Beaver Hole as Corey was on his way out. 

Went to the end of the line to see the gnome, then back to Corey's line so I could have a look at his lead. Flow is so strong that I couldn't tell that Corey had been in there hauling big rocks <10mins before because the lead was crystal clear. Access to lead is very good now too - would have been able to squeeze in easily but didn't because Daz was waiting. 

Came out and met Daz, then indicated to head out. Daz followed the white line straight into the Pagey Pocket - swam up, grabbed him and pulled back, then wrote on wet notes "That's the Pagey Pocket - very tight" and he nodded then went back in! I stayed well clear near the line junction, and after a few minutes I could hear him calling to me through his regulator - he was stuck and needed help. Followed the line in, found him, guided him out, and then directed him back onto the orange line to exit the cave.
- **Lesson:** Restrictions can turn equipment choices into exit problems; keep kit appropriate and preserve options.
- **Humour:** Potential comedy in hindsight: the undignified physics of trying to reverse a cave diver out of a restriction.
- **Wonder:** 
- **Links:** Pines, Mount Gambier, Corey Auld, Matt Gebert, Darren Mitchell (Daz), Risk, Humour
- **Book relevance:** 
- **Chapter candidate:** 
- **Follow-up notes:**

### Cave Dive #486 — 2025-05-03 — Pines, Mount Gambier

- **Metadata:** duration `85:40`, max depth `34.8 m`, buddy `Peter Alexander, Darryl Lowndes`, Subsurface dive `909`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 28709 minutes (486)
- **Story:** Crazy Wombat Tunnel - Darryl felt narked/pressured so stayed in CCR again, Peter carried X4 camera all the way to CCR then handed off to me before going through GADI Gap. Filmed to the end of the line, then relaid exploration line (with a gap) into lead on left hand side of rockpile - very scary, got super stuck, but very obvious flow coming from NW.
- **Lesson:** Restrictions can turn equipment choices into exit problems; keep kit appropriate and preserve options.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.
- **Humour:** Potential comedy in hindsight: the undignified physics of trying to reverse a cave diver out of a restriction.

- **Metadata:** duration `57:30`, max depth `20.1 m`, buddy `Peter Alexander, Darryl Lowndes`, Subsurface dive `911`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 28837 minutes (488)
- **Story:** First dive in EEX with the Kiwis. Darryl stayed in the initial Englebrecht's East air chamber, Pete and I pushed through to the extension - dived full length of West, then jumped down to southern branch and turned around at Doormouse. Placed one of Pete's cookies just past the doormouse restriction, then came back. 

Definitely lots of potential leads left to explore in there - should try to go back with good lights and do a full check of all the various holes along the Southern branch.

- **Metadata:** duration `84:10`, max depth `14.3 m`, buddy `Yoshitaka Ishaji`, Subsurface dive `959`
- **Total cave time line:** Total Cave Time: 30822 minutes (517)
- **Story:** Surveyed in with MNemo (wheel not recording distances!), reached 4th chamber, photos of Explorers Club flag under cave bacon, measured distances by hand on exit - lost Explorer's Club flag somewhere on way back
- **Lesson:** Exploration becomes more valuable when it is measured, mapped, and made legible for others.; The appeal of cave diving often lives in the possibility that the next metre is unknown.; Symbols matter because they connect a single dive to a much larger tradition of exploration.
- **Humour:** Potential comedy: losing an Explorers Club flag after photographing it like a sacred artefact.; Potential comedy: expensive/clever survey technology immediately defeated by reality.
- **Wonder:** Making the invisible cave visible through survey and mapping.; Visual wonder / cave formations.; Explorer’s Club Flag moment; connects the dive to a wider exploration lineage.; International cave exploration arc leading toward Laos.
